[Table_InudstryRankInfo] 行业投资评级 
强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。

先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

コメント要旨出典
耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して
いると報告ベージュブック
（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め
を試みたが、供給業者は他案件への供給を優先
し、これを拒否
機械製造業は、DC向けに鋼材在庫が大量に消
費されるなか、鉄鋼メーカーによる供給制限が他
の建設案件の遅延を招いていると指摘
テキサス州
見通し調査
（2026/9）
製造業では原材料コストが上昇。特に鋼材価格
が急上昇しており、その一因はDC需要とされるベージュブック
（2026/9） 一部の製造業がアルミニウム・銅・鋼材の供給不
足や調達期間の長期化を報告
一部の自動車メーカーはアルミニウムや一部部品
の供給不足で生産が幾分抑制されたと報告

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

（年／月）
日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移
（注） 食品は生鮮、コメ及び外食を除く食料価格
（出所） 総務省「消費者物価指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
（注） 緑線は宿泊料、私立高校授業料を除く系列
（出所） 総務省「消費者物価指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
原油高等の価格転嫁が進展し、
その他財を中心に加速 基調的なサービス
価格にも上昇圧力
注目点②サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線
（年/月）

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

基本構想」 より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

げ等の実態に関する調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

Document 080: 2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 ～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年10 月 22 日

1/5 
2027 年春闘、初動のモメンタムは現時点で良好 
～労働側、経営側とも昨年からのトーンダウンはみられず～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 連合は2027 年春闘基本構想で、 定昇込みの賃上げ要求を 「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベア等の
賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とする目安を示した。前年よりも要求姿勢をやや強めて
おり、５％台の賃上げの定着に加え、さらなる引き上げを目指す姿勢がうかがえる。 
○ 経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経労委報告の骨子案では賃上げ
モメンタムの「浸透・定着」が掲げられ、高い賃上げの継続に加え、中小企業への波及を重
視する姿勢が示されている。 
○ 日本経済研究センターのＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査によると、民間エコノミストは 2027 年
春闘の賃上げ率を4.96％と予想（厚生労働省ベース）。前年から小幅に鈍化するものの、高
い伸びが続く見通しであり、４年連続で５％台の賃上げを実現できるかが焦点となる。 
○ 春先にはイラン情勢悪化による賃上げ鈍化が懸念されていたが、現時点で労使双方の姿勢に
トーンダウンはみられない。企業業績の底堅さや構造的な人手不足が高い賃上げを支えてお
り、2027 年春闘の初動のモメンタムは良好と評価できる。

連合は、「６％中心、５％以上」と昨年からトーンをやや強める 
2027 年春闘の集中回答日は2027 年の３月中旬だが、実際の交渉に向けての動きは既に始まりつ
つある。 
本日、連合は「2027 春季生活闘争基本構想」を公表した。春闘に向けて最初に注目される重要
イベントであり、翌年の春闘に向けた労働組合側の基本理念や方針、重点課題を示すものであ
る。この基本構想をみることで、労働側が翌年の春闘にどの程度強気な姿勢で臨もうとしている
かを早い段階で確認することができ、春闘全体のトーンをつかむうえで重要な材料となる。 
今回の基本構想の最大の注目点は、定期昇給相当分を含めて「６％中心、５％以上」 、ベースア
ップ等の賃上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、より高い賃上げを目指す姿勢を示したことで
ある。「５％以上」という要求水準自体は４年連続（2023 年は「５％程度」）となるが、今回は
「６％中心」との表現が新たに加わった。ベア等の賃上げ分についても、従来の「３％以上」に

### Page 2

2/5 
「４％中心」が加えられている。前年の2026 年春闘では、全体で「５％以上」 、中小で「６％以
上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者では「７％」を目安とする、いわゆる「５・６・７」の要求が
示されていた。今回、全体についても６％を明確に意識する形となったことは、実質的には５％
を最低ラインとしつつ、より高い賃上げを促す姿勢を強めたものと受け取れる。単に高い賃上げ
を続けるだけでなく、その水準をさらに引き上げようとする意欲がうかがえる。

こうした姿勢の背景にあるのが、ここ数年の高い賃上げ実績である。2026 年春闘では連合ベー
スの賃上げ率の実績が5.01％となり、３年連続で５％台の高い賃上げが実現した。５％という水
準がかつてほど実現困難な目標ではなくなりつつあるなか、連合としても前年と同じ要求目安を
繰り返すだけでは、さらなる賃上げを促すメッセージとして十分ではないと判断した可能性があ
る。５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持するだけでなく、６％近辺を意識して賃上げ率のさらなる引
き上げを目指す段階に入ったというメッセージと位置付けられる。 
賃上げを取り巻く環境も、こうした強気の姿勢を支えている。構造的な人手不足が続くなか、
企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き重要な経営課題であり、賃金引き上げの必要性は高
い。また、物価上昇率は2026 年度後半にかけて再び高まるとみられ、家計の生活コスト負担は重
い。名目賃金が増加しても、物価上昇に追いつかなければ生活水準の改善にはつながらない。実
質賃金を安定的にプラスにするためにも、労働側が高い賃上げを求める理由は十分にある。 
加えて、賃上げの広がりという点でも課題は残る。2026 年春闘では中小組合の賃上げ率が
4.69％と高い伸びを確保したものの、全体の5.01％には届かなかった。大企業と中小企業の間に
はなお差があり、正規雇用と非正規雇用の賃金格差も残っている。連合にとっては、高い賃上げ
春闘年 連合の要求目安（定昇相当分込み） 連合ベース 厚労省ベース
2020 ４％程度 1.90％ 2.00％
2021 ４％程度 1.78％ 1.86％
2022 ４％程度 2.07％ 2.20％
2023 ５％程度 3.58％ 3.60％
2024 ５％以上 5.10％ 5.33％
2025 ５％以上 5.25％ 5.52％
2026 ５％以上 5.01％ 5.18％
2027 ６％中心、５％以上 — —
(出所）連合「春季生活闘争基本構想」、連合「春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結果」、厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」
連合の賃上げ要求目安と春闘賃上げ率
実績

### Page 3

3/5 
を実現するだけでなく、それを幅広い企業や労働者に波及させることも引き続き重要な課題であ
る。今回の基本構想でも、賃金実態を把握できないなどの事情がある中小組合については
「20,000 円以上・６％以上」 、有期・短時間・契約等労働者については「７％」を目安とする方針
が示された。こうした点を踏まえれば、今回の基本構想は、高い賃上げの持続と一段の引き上
げ、そして賃上げの裾野の拡大を目指すものと評価できる。 
また、今回の基本構想では、新たに2030 年の賃金水準を2026 年比で15％程度引き上げる中期
目標も設定された。毎年の賃上げ率だけでなく、中期的に目指す賃金水準を明示することで、高
い賃上げを一過性のものに終わらせず、継続させようとする姿勢がうかがえる。 
なお、近年は個別組合の実際の賃上げ要求率がすでに６％近くに達しており、 「６％中心」とい
う今回の表現には、こうした実態を追認する意味合いもある。今回の基本構想からは、連合の強
い賃上げ意欲が確認できる一方、実際の賃上げ率が前年を上回るかどうかはまた別問題である。
今後は、主要産業別労働組合組織や個別組合の要求水準が前年からどの程度引き上げられるか
（られないか）が注目される。

経営側も賃上げの継続に前向き 
経営側も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。経団連が毎年１月に公表する「経
営労働政策特別委員会報告（経労委報告） 」は、春闘に向けた経営側の基本スタンスを示すもので
あり、賃上げ交渉の方向性を占う上で重要な資料となる。2027 年春闘に向けた経労委報告につい
ては、現時点で原案は明らかになっていないものの、９月上旬には骨子案の内容が報道されてい
る。それによると、賃上げモメンタムの「浸透・定着」を掲げ、高い賃上げの継続と中小企業へ
の波及を促す方針とされる。 
経団連は2023 年を賃上げの「起点」 、2024 年を「加速」 、2025 年を「定着」 、2026 年を「さらな
る定着」の年と位置付けてきた。今回の「浸透・定着」という表現からは、高い賃上げを継続す
るとともに、その動きを中小企業などに広げることを重視する姿勢がうかがえる。また、経団連
の筒井会長は９月７日の記者会見で、 「ベースアップ実施の検討を引き続きスタンダードと位置付
けていきたい」と述べ、賃上げのモメンタムを維持する考えを示している。 
数か月前には、イラン情勢の悪化による原材料費や物流費の上昇が企業収益を圧迫し、2027 年
春闘では経営側が賃上げに慎重な姿勢を強めることも懸念されていた。しかし、現時点で経営側
の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられない。 
なお、経労委報告では、原案から正式な報告にかけての修正は例年あまり見られないが、骨子
案から原案にかけては内容が多少修正されることもある。今後、10 月末～11 月中頃までに報道さ
れるとみられる経労委報告の原案が明らかになれば、経営側のスタンスをより具体的に判断でき
るだろう。

エコノミスト予想は4.96％、５％を巡る攻防か 
10 月14 日に日本経済研究センターが公表した「ＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査」では、民間エコノ

### Page 4

4/5 
ミスト 34 人を対象に2027 年春闘の賃上げ率について特別調査を実施している。これによると、
予測値の平均は4.96％となり、2026 年実績の5.18％を 0.22％ポイント下回る見通しとなった。
もっとも、前年から小幅に鈍化するとはいえ、ほぼ５％という歴史的に高い水準であり、2027 年
も高い賃上げが続くとの見方が中心である。なお、ここでの賃上げ率は厚生労働省「民間主要企
業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」ベースであり、連合の集計とは対象が異なる。 
予測の内訳をみると、定期昇給分が1.69％、ベースアップ分が3.27％となっており、ベアにつ
いても３％を超える伸びが見込まれている。また、予測値の高位８機関平均は5.23％、低位８機
関平均は4.68％となっている。予測には一定の幅があるものの、高い賃上げが続くという点では
概ね認識は共有されている。

2027 年春闘で賃上げ率が前年からやや鈍化すると予想される背景には、2026 年の物価上昇率が
2025 年より低く、物価高に対応するための賃上げ圧力が幾分弱まることがあると考えられる。ま
た、数年にわたる高い賃上げによって人件費負担が累積していることに加え、イラン情勢悪化を
背景とした原材料費や物流費の上昇も、企業の賃上げ余力を抑制する要因となり得る。一方、企
業業績が足元まで好調を維持していることや、構造的な人手不足と採用競争の激化は引き続き賃
上げを促す要因であり、賃上げ率が大きく鈍化する可能性も低いとみられる。 
ちなみに、昨年の同時期のＥＳＰフォーキャスト調査でも、2026 年春闘の賃上げ率は4.81％
（2025 年10 月調査）と、前年実績からの鈍化が予想されていた。しかし、実際には5.18％と予
想を上回り、３年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現した。今回も前年からの小幅な鈍化が見込まれて
いるが、予測平均は昨年の同時期をやや上回っている。イラン情勢の悪化という新たな懸念材料
があるなかでも、エコノミストの間では高い賃上げが続くとの見方が維持されていることが分か
（出所）日本経済研究センター「ESPフォーキャスト調査（2026年10月調査）」
（注）2027年は予測値、2026年までの実績は厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結状況」

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
る。 
こうしたなか、予測平均の4.96％について、前年からの「鈍化」を強調するか、5％近傍という
「高水準の持続」を重視するかは、評価が分かれるところだろう。いずれにせよ、2027 年春闘で
は、３年連続で実現した５％台の賃上げを４年目も維持できるか、５％を巡る攻防が一つの焦点
となる。今後、経労委報告原案の判明により経営側の方針が明らかになるほか、主要産業別労働
組合組織の要求方針などが明らかになるにつれて、賃上げ率の見通しもより具体化していくこと
になるだろう。 
なお、この調査とは別に、毎年11 月～12 月にかけて、民間シンクタンク・調査機関から翌年の
春闘賃上げ率の予測が出されることが多い （筆者は 11 月に発表予定） 。 予測をレポートとして作成
することもあれば、 経済見通しレポートのなかで言及されることもあり、春闘賃上げ率の見通しに
ついて、この時期に市場の相場観が徐々に形成されていく。

2027 年春闘の初動は良好 
以上を踏まえると、2027 年春闘の「初動のモメンタム」は、現時点では良好と評価できる。イ
ラン情勢が悪化した数か月前には、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益の圧迫を通じて、
2027 年春闘の賃上げ率が大きく鈍化するとの懸念もあった。しかし、ここまでの動きをみる限
り、労働側、経営側のいずれについても、前年と比べて賃上げ姿勢が慎重化している様子はみら
れない。連合が定昇込み、ベアともに前年より強い要求目安を打ち出し、さらに高い賃上げを志
向する一方、経団連も高い賃上げの継続と中小企業への波及に前向きな姿勢を示している。 
その背景には、原油などの代替調達が進み、資源の調達難が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が
回避されたことや、ＡＩ需要の拡大にも支えられ、企業業績が総じて堅調さを維持していること
があるとみられる。また、構造的な人手不足や採用競争の激化によって、企業が人材確保・定着
のために賃金を引き上げる必要性は依然として高い。物価上昇が続くなか、実質賃金の改善を求
める声が強いことも、高い賃上げに向けての動きを後押ししているとみられる。 
もっとも、原材料費や物流費の上昇による企業収益への悪影響が今後さらに広がる可能性はあ
り、先行きを楽観視することはできない。また、現時点で確認できるのは労使双方の基本的な姿
勢であり、具体的な要求や回答の水準はまだ明らかになっていない。今後は、経団連「経労委報
告」の原案や主要産業別労働組合組織の要求方針、早期に賃上げを表明する企業の動向などを通
じて、今回確認された前向きな姿勢が実際の賃上げにつながるかを見極める必要がある。

### Page 1

Poya  (5904.TWO)
Strong 3Q as Expected; Tougher Comparisons Ahead
CITI'S TAKE 
Poya’s 3Q26 net profit +26% y/y came largely in-line with our forecast, and 
5% ahead of BBG consensus. SSSG of 7.0%, higher GPM, opex savings and 
stronger op leverage lifted OPM to 17.5%, the key drivers to strong 
profitability. Looking into 4Q, tougher comp should moderate sales and 
earnings growth despite fast store additions and solid operation execution. 
We maintain Neutral as the strong share price rally YTD appears to reflect 
most positives from robust SSSG and margin expansion. A higher comp 
base and potentially intensifying competition limit the room for further re-
rating. 
3Q26 broadly in-line; solid margins boosted by strong op leverage — Poya 
reported 3Q26 net profits of NT$1.0bn +26% y/y, largely in-line with our est and 5% 
above BBG’s est. 3Q sales of NT$7.4bn +15% y/y were supported by solid SSSG 
+7.0% y/y with ticket size +4.9% and traffic +2.0%. Higher GPM, strong opex 
savings and op leverage lifted OPM to 17.5%, topping our/BBG est of 17.1%/16.6%. 
T otal store count reached 495 at end-3Q26 (+25 net increase in 9M26). 
4Q outlook: faster store expansion meets a higher comp base — We expect 30 net 
store additions in 4Q26E vs. 35 stores prior, bringing the year-end store count to 
525 and supporting continued sales growth. Demand for beauty-related products 
remains healthy. However, the elevated comparison base from 4Q26E onward 
should moderate SSSG and overall sales and earnings growth, despite the 
contribution from new stores. We believe this potential growth deceleration could 
temper investor enthusiasm and trigger profit-taking pressure.
Implications: Slightly positive reaction expected; maintain Neutral — We keep our 
earnings estimates for 26-28E largely unchanged in view of the in-line 3Q results. 
We expect a slightly positive market reaction to the results given the healthy margin 
expansion. However, strong sales had likely raised expectations for 3Q profitability, 
limiting the scope for a more meaningful positive surprise. Our Neutral thesis 
remains unchanged – we believe the strong share price rally has priced in much of 
the benefit from robust SSSG and margin improvement. Looking ahead, tougher 
comp could constrain growth, while potentially intensifying competition, including 
Cosmed’s introduction of Olive Young products, could challenge Poya’s beauty-led 
momentum. We therefore see limited room for further re-rating despite solid 
operating execution.

Poya  (5904.TWO)

5
Poya
Company description
Poya is the largest retailer of fast-moving consumer goods in Taiwan, focusing
on low-priced cosmetics, accessories, clothing and food products for females
aged 15-49. Poya had 470 stores in Taiwan at the end of 2025 and believes
that the market can accommodate up to 700-800 stores before reaching
saturation.
Investment strategy
We have a Neutral rating on Poya. We think YTD rally has baked in most

positives, including strong SSSG and solid margin expansion. While
fundamentals remain solid, tougher comparisons from 4Q26E could
moderate this year's 6-7% SSSG, lowering operating leverage and capping
further earnings upside. Meanwhile, Cosmed's expanding rollout of Olive
Young brands is intensifying competition for beauty spending. Although
Poya's SSSG has remained resilient, braoder distribution of these brands
could divert customer traffic and increase promotional pressure. We continue

to recognize Poya's execution in store expansion and ongoing efficiency
improvements. However, with these strengths largely reflected in the share
price and growth momentum facing a higher hurdle from 4Q26E onward, we
see a more balanced risk/reward.
Valuation
We derive our 12-month target price of NT$70 for Poya from a DCF valuation
method. Our assumptions include a terminal growth rate of 4.5% and WACC
of 10.1% (beta of 1.05; risk-free rate of 4%; risk premium of 7.0%; cost of equity

of 11.3%). Our target price implies a P/E of 18x on our NTM EPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: intense price competition among online retailers, slower store traffic,
margin dilution due to aggressive store expansion, and a soft macro
environment.
Key upside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: easing price competition among online retailers, stronger store

traffic, margin expansion thanks to strong cost control, and a stronger macro
environment.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

### Page 1

M
Idea
3Q GM likely to decline on higher costs and slower new product adoption…
Lotes reported unaudited 3Q26 revenue of NT$9,924M (+4% q/q, +18% y/y). Higher
copper, gold and plastics costs for legacy products, coupled with slower new
product adoption, should continue to pressure GM. We forecast 3Q GM of 44.6% (-
90bps q/q), below consensus of 45.8% (+30bps q/q). However, a decline in opex to
NT$2.0B, consistent with management commentary, should support operating

profit growth of 17% q/q to NT$2,452M, albeit still 6% below consensus. We
forecast NP of NT$2,174M (+10% q/q) and EPS of NT$19.36, 4% below the Street.
…but we see 3Q as the GM trough, with recovery beginning in 4Q: Following
slower  server platform transitions, we expect adoption to accelerate from 4Q26.
Socket production for AMD's Venice CPU will ramp from 4Q26 and socket
production for Intel’s Oak Stream CPU will ramp in 1Q27. These transitions should

enable Lotes to reprice products and pass through higher input costs, supporting
margin recovery. Our industry checks also point to a rebound in ODM server board
production in 4Q, following a modest 3Q decline. We forecast 4Q revenue of ~NT
$10.5B (+5% q/q), GM of 45.9% (+130bps q/q) and EPS of NT$21.11 (+9% q/q).
2027 - margin recovery and new AI product ramps should drive earnings growth:
Rising adoption of new CPU platforms should support socket margin recovery,

alongside growing contributions from QD, SOCAMM and NPO. QD reached 2–3% of
revenue in September, with further growth expected in 4Q26 and 2027, and we
forecast SOCAMM will reach ~7% of revenue in CY27. Lotes has two NPO
opportunities, one through LINTES and another directly, with the direct program
expected to ramp in 2H27. Although initial contributions should remain modest, we
see NPO as a longer-term AI data center growth opportunity and a potential re-

rating catalyst. Overall, we see the AI revenue mix, including QD, SOCAMM and
NPO, rising from ~5% in CY25 to slightly above 20% by CY28.
Upgrade to OW and raise PT by 15% to NT$2,350, driven by our estimate increases
for CY27-28. Our new PT implies 22x 2027e P/E and 16.5x 2028e P/E, above the past
3-year average of 17.4x, which we view as reasonable amid a rising AI revenue mix.

3Q26 Results Preview
3Q26 preview:  We forecast revenue of NT$9.92B (+4% q/q, +18% y/y), broadly in line
with the Street. Although gross margin should decline 80bps q/q to 44.6%, a 12%
sequential reduction in operating expenses should lift operating margin 270bps q/q to
24.7%. Nevertheless, this remains 130bps below consensus, implying operating profit of
NT$2.45bn, 6% below the Street. We therefore see potential for an earnings miss, as we

estimate net profit of ~NT$2,174M (+10% q/q, -6% y/y), for EPS of NT$19.36, 4% below
the Street.

6
Price Target Discussion and Valuation Methodology
Our price target increases ~15%, to NT$2,350 from NT$2,045, driven mainly by our
higher earnings estimates for 2027-28.
Our new PT implies ~22x 2027e P/E, slightly above its three-year average forward P/E of
17.4x, but still below that of peer Amphenol (25-35x 2027e P/E, based on Visible Alpha
consensus). We think Lotes stock could  re-rate to a higher valuation multiple if margins
hold up well and Lotes' exposure to AI PCs and AI servers grows in the coming years.

We continue to derive our PT, which is our base case scenario value, from a residual
income model (RIM), consistent with the methodology we apply to the rest of our tech
hardware coverage universe. To calculate the intrinsic value of the stock, we use the
company's beginning equity plus the present value of all expected residual income of
earnings in excess of the cost of capital. The RI is positive when ROAE is above the cost of
capital and negative when it is below the cost of capital.

Our key RIM assumptions (all unchanged) include a cost of equity of 9.2% (beta of ~1.0,
equity risk premium of ~8.7% and risk-free rate of ~1.0%), a medium-term growth rate of
12.5% and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Our bull and bear case values rise to NT$3,400 and NT1,285, respectively, in line with
the increase in magnitude of our base case scenario.

▪ We believe GM will trough in 3Q and start
to recover from 4Q onward, driven by rising
adoption rates of new server platform CPU
sockets, which help Lotes reﬂect a higher

for AI servers have been rising since 2H26

in CY27 in small volume, with increases in
2028.

from ~5% in CY5 to slightly more than 20%
by CY28.

undemanding vs. its current valuation of 17x

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
6770 力積電(Powerchip)

報告日期： 2026/10/22 研 究 員：羅  金 鳳 (Sunny Lo)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 在記憶體及邏輯產品價格季增 20% 的帶動下，使整體毛
利率跳升 19 個百分點 ， 來到47.69% ，單 季 稅 後EPS 1.38 元，
優於預期。  
 4Q26 將反映晶圓投片及產出的時間差，使出貨量及 ASP 將再
增加，帶動營收、毛利率雙位升。  
 AI 帶動矽電容需求大爆發，目前力積電產能僅 1 萬片 /月，但
2027 年需求將達 3～5 萬片 / 月。

一. 反映投片與產出落差， 4Q26 營收、毛利率雙升  
力積電 3Q26 晶圓出貨量 41.6 萬片， QoQ-1.44% ，主因為銅鑼廠
機器搬遷所致，且 3D AI Foundry 營收占比也因遷廠而由 2Q26
的 5.4% 下滑到 1.4%， 但因記憶體及邏輯產品價格持續上漲 ，ASP
季增 20% ，使力積電 3Q26 整體營收達 212.78 億元，
QoQ+23.07% ，YoY+79.71% ，在價格上漲及產能利用率提升的帶
動下，整體毛利率由 2Q26 的 28.28% 大幅上升至 47.69% ，稅後
EPS 1.38 元，優於預期。

由於力積電投片與產出的落差 30%( 邏輯遞延 2～3 個月， DRAM
遞延 4 個月 )，因此， 4Q26 的晶圓出貨及 ASP 將比 3Q26 更高，
加上 ， 銅鑼廠的機器設備已完成搬遷到新竹廠 ，Interposer 及 WoW
的產能將在 11～12 月逐步恢復生產，其中 Interposer 將於 2027
年達到滿載。綜上，我們預估力積電 4Q26 營收 234.3 億元，
QoQ+10.11% ，YoY+87.51% ，毛利率進一步提升至 51.81% ，單
季稅後 EPS 1.64 元，合計 2026 年營收 755.71 億元，
YoY+61.72% ，在記憶體價格大漲的帶動下，毛利率由 2025 年的
-3.29% 跳升至 37.8% ，稅後 EPS 6.58 元(含售廠給美光 )。

二. 2027 年矽電容需求大爆發  
力積電營運轉型將陸續展現成果，美光授權生產的 DRAM 的 1P
製程，將於 2026 完成 PDK ，2Q27 試產， 2028 年有機會貢獻全
年營收； HBM 的後段製程代工 PWF ，由美光提供設備， 2026 年
試產， 4Q27 量產， 2028 年貢獻全年營收； 3D AI Foundry 方面，
堆疊技術將由 Micro bump 進入到 Hybrid bonding ，以及
Interposer 的放量生產 ， 在外溢效果持續下 ，2027 年產能將滿載；
Wafer on Wafer 方面，在銅鑼廠設千完全搬遷至新竹廠後，目前
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：95 元 
     11 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：66.2 元 
     7.67 X 2027 EPS 
     2026/10/21 收盤

Company Report 
公司研究報告

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

2 
已小量試產， 1H27 將正式量產。由於建廠及買購設備需大額資本
支出，風險相對較高，因此， PWF 由美光提供設備，未來的 WoW
也將會採取輕資產的模式，降低營運風險，不過，因大廠均將產
能移去生產高階 DRAM ，38nm 、25nm 、20nm 這些傳統 DRAM
製程未來全球將只剩力積電生產，預估此一需求將長達 10～15
年。

此外 ， 因AI 晶片的高功耗及高速傳輸需求 ， 為提升電源傳輸效率 、
降低電阻、去雜訊等問題，將帶動 IPD 需求大爆發，力積電目前
以現有 DRAM 的 38nm、25nm 製程做生產 IPD，恰好目前 DRAM
產能供不應求的情況下 ，IPD 的產能只能配置約 1 萬片 /月，而2027
年全球 IPD 的需求將達 3～5 萬片 /月，且能見度已延伸至 2028
年，顯見需求遠超過力積電所能提供，因此，力積電已在尋找建
廠用地，且將聯合策略客戶共同投資新產能。

三. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 8.63 元 
AI 進入到推理後對記憶體已需求量更甚以往，但因全球記憶體產
能嚴重供不應求的情況下， GUP 大廠將 12 層 HBM 降低到 8 層，
顯見記憶體緊俏情況嚴峻，也使記憶體價格易漲難跌，預估 2027
年記憶體價格仍至少維持在高點，加上成熟製程處在有利的價格
環境， 2026 年漲價雙位數後， 2027 年將再漲 10～20% ，因此，
我們預估力積電 2027 年營收 1223.38 億元， YoY+61.89% ，毛利
率 49.89% ，稅後 EPS 8.63 元。

四. 投資建議為買進，目標價 95 元 
受惠 AI 帶動的記憶體漲價潮持續， 2026 年力積電將呈現轉虧為
盈，且 2026 及 2027 年獲利都將大幅成長，加上銅鑼廠售予美光
且變成長期的代工合作夥伴關係，有利力積電長期營運穩定性，
也解決力積電資產負債表壓力，因此，投資建議為買進，目標價
95 元(11X2027 年稅後 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
力晶集團 05/2019 完成企業重組 ， 由旗下的力晶科技3 座 12 吋晶
圓廠及相關營業、資產，讓與力晶積成電子製造股份有限公司 (簡
稱“力積電” (6770)) 。力積電為聚焦專業晶圓代工、產業定位明
確，目前擁有 2 座 8 吋及 3 座 12 吋晶圓廠，將針對先進記憶體、
客製化邏輯積體電路與分離式元件的三大晶圓代工服務主軸，持
續 Open Foundry 營運模式，從晶片設計、製造服務，到設備、

公司研究報告

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

3 
產能分享，根據不同客戶的屬性和需求，共同建立緊密、彈性的
合作機制，且著眼半導體 3D 堆疊產業大趨勢，力積電與國際知名
大客戶密切配合開發 3D 堆疊封裝，將成為公司長線另一成長動
能。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1、佈局 3D IC 為公司未來運重要利基 
力積電有部份產品屬於記憶體產品，營運雖然將隨著市場波動，
受產業景氣循環大，然力積電善用其技術優勢，將其記憶體產品
與國際知名大廠合作，延伸至 3D 堆疊封裝技術，僅需利用舊製程
即可將晶片效能數倍提升，其以 55nm 的邏輯 IC 堆疊 38nm 的
DRAM 製程，封裝後的晶片用於挖礦機，效能較現有的解決方案
大幅提升 9 倍。由 於 透 過3D 堆疊可使晶片設計效率提高， 提升晶
片整體效能，減少晶片尺寸，加快晶片內部元件溝通速度，為半
導體先進封裝未來重要發展趨勢，且因先進封裝技術成本不斷提
升，客戶為考量使用的成本效益，未來將有更多廠商使用 3D 堆疊
的解決方案取代先進製程技術 ， 使得3D 堆疊技術長線發展具極大
潛力，力積電長線著眼於此一產業大趨勢，將積極深化此一佈局，
為公司長線營運提供良好動能。

2、四大營運方向引領公司未來成長動能 
力積電在經過 2019 年 3 座 8 吋廠及 2 座 12 吋廠的整併，加上邏
輯技術逐漸步上軌道後，使其在 40nm ～0.11um 的技術於全球站
穩腳步，具有指標地位。公司除將以此為基礎拓展多元應用，在
新技術方面亦不斷投入研發，展望未來，擬定四大方向引領公司
成長動能： 1、發展更先進的技術 --- 第四代半導體氧化銦鎵鋅
(IGZO) ，因 IGZO 的材料特性，其超低功耗的性能，僅為目前耗
電的 1/20， 及顯示器解析度能超過5,000DDIP ， 有助應用於AR/VR
的裝置有較佳續航力及超高解析度，使用者將完全沈浸於元宇宙
世界。 2、3D-Interchip 堆疊技術：與晶圓代工大廠台積電合作，
將記憶體與邏輯 IC 進行 3D 堆疊 ， 使晶片具更小體積 、 更低功耗，
更低成本，及開發更有效率。 3D 堆疊的 Wafer on Wafer 目前以
供應挖礦機需求為主，未來將專注於 AI 龐大需求。 3、Interchip ：
將邏輯元件直接放入記憶體元件，以提高記憶體效能，適用於
IoT 、AI 及邊緣運算。 4、車用電子晶片：因應節能減碳的趨勢，
電動車 / 新能源車將快速發展，公司將致力推進第三代半導體
GaN、SiC 技術製程，應用於 PMIC 。由於 PMIC 為車汽應用的重
要零組件之一，且 PMIC 的製造非用最先進的半導體製程，力積
電目前的製程範圍恰好符合最佳生產製程的節點，而電動車的興
起將帶動 PMIC 的應用大幅成長，加上 AI 趨勢確立下，對 PMIC
有更高需求，為公司未來重要佈局方向及營運重要成長動能。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.64 元 
由於力積電投片與產出的落差 30%( 邏輯遞延 2～3 個月， DRAM
遞延 4 個月 )，因此， 4Q26 的晶圓出貨及 ASP 將比 3Q26 更高，
加上銅鑼廠的機器設備已完成搬遷到新竹廠， Interposer 及 WoW
的產能將在 11～12 月逐步恢復生產，其中 Interposer 將於 2027
年達到滿載。綜上，我們預估力積電 4Q26 營收 234.3 億元，
QoQ+10.11% ，YoY+87.51% ，毛利率進一步提升至 51.81% ，單
季稅後 EPS 1.64 元。

2、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 8.63 
AI 進入到推理後對記憶體已需求量更甚以往，但因全球記憶體產
能嚴重供不應求的情況下， GUP 大廠將 12 層 HBM 降低到 8 層，
顯見記憶體緊俏情況嚴峻，也使記憶體價格易漲難跌，預估 2027
年記憶體價格仍至少維持在高點，加上成熟製程處在有利的價格
環境， 2026 年漲價雙位數後， 2027 年將再漲 10～20% ，因此，
我們預估力積電 2027 年營收 1223.38 億元， YoY+61.89% ，毛利
率 49.89% ，稅後 EPS 8.63 元。

2026.10.30   
한화에어로스페이스  
(012450)

3Q26: 그래도 믿는다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원 
한화에어로스페이스의 3분기 매출은 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원(OPM 7.4%)로 
컨센서스와 당사 추정치를 모두 대폭 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다. 앞서 실적을 발표한 
한화오션의 실적 쇼크를 감안하더라도, 주력사업인 지상방산 부문의 실적이 매출 1조 
2,455억원/영업이익 217억원(1.7%)로 지난 분기(2조 1,075억원/ 5,330억원/ 25.3%) 
는 물론 전년동기(2조 1,098억원/5,725억원/27.1%)보다도 현저히 감소하였다. 
일회성 요인은 대전사업장 폭발사고에 따른 복구비용 등 300억원, 일부 국내사업 
충당금 160억원 및 환율하락에 따른 효과 등이 있었으나 역시 이를 감안해도 내수와 
수출 모두 실질 이익률은 5%미만으로 추정된다. 지상방산 분기 매출은 1분기(1조 
2,211억)보다 오히려 소폭 증가하였으나 영업이익이 1분기 2,087억원에서 
217억원으로 대폭 감소한 점에 대하여 회사는 프로젝트별 믹스 구성에 따른 낮은 
이익률의 인도분이 반영되었다고 설명하고 있으나, 그럼에도 불구하고 수출과 내수 
이익률 모두 BEP에 가까웠다는 점은 쉽게 이해가 가지는 않는다. 다만 방산기업의 
특성상 대전사업장 사고에 따른 실제 납기 영향 등 회사가 공개할 수 없는 사정이 
있었을 것으로 추측할 뿐이다. 대전사업장은 현재 복구작업이 진행 중이며 27년 
상반기 중에는 가동 정상화할 것으로 예상된다.  
긍정적인 소식도 있다. 폴란드향 천무의 연간 인도대수가 기존 가이던스인 40대에서 
60대로 증가하였으며, 이를 포함하여 3분기에 인도되지 않은 물량의 대부분이 
4분기에 집중될 예정으로, 올해의 4분기 실적 개선폭은 통상적으로 볼 수 있는 
4분기의 계절적 집중도보다 더 커질 것으로 예상한다. 방산기업의 실적은 인도일정에 
따라 크게 변할 수 있는 분기 실적보다 연간 합산이 더 중요하다는 점을 한 번 더 
믿고, 4분기를 기다리는 게 좋겠다.

투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 1,200,000원 하향  
투자의견 BUY를 유지하지만, SOTP기반 목표주가는 기존 1,490,000원에서 
1,200,000원으로 19.5% 하향한다. 비영업가치 중 자회사 한화시스템과 한화오션, 
쎄트렉아이의 지분가치 하락(합산 8.7조원→7.1조원), 그리고 지난 분기 대비 늘어난 
별도 순차입금(3.25조→4.95조원)을 반영하였으며 최근 방산섹터 전반의 밸류 하락을 
감안하여 영업가치 중 지상방산+항공우주의 Target P/E를 기존 35배에서 30배로 
조정하였다. 
3분기는 여러모로 악재가 겹쳤다. 주식시장 전반의 하락에 더해 방산섹터 수주 지연에 
따른 디레이팅, 그리고 납기 지연에 따른 실적 하락 등 나올 수 있는 악재는 다 
나왔다고도 볼 수 있다. 그러나 뒤집어보면 지연중인 수주는 시간의 문제일 뿐 가시화 
가능성이 매우 높으며, 실적 또한 진행률 인식이 아닌 인도기준 인식에 따른 일시적 
현상으로 4분기 이후 복구될 가능성이 크다. 펀더멘털의 유의미한 변화는 없다고 볼 
수 있으며 밸류에이션 매력도가 증가한 현 시점에서 추가 매수를 고려할 때다. 다만, 
대전사업장 사고에 따른 당국의 제재조치가 아직 확정되지 않았음은 잔여 리스크 
요인이다. 동종사의 신규계약 입찰제한 제재조치가 주가에 상당한 영향을 미쳤음을 
고려하면 한화에어로스페이스 또한 제재조치 여부를 지켜본 후 투자할 것을 권한다.

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
 당 연구원은 한화에어로스페이스(012450)의 일부 비용을 지원받아 한화에어로스페이스 공장 투어(2026년 10월 1-2)를 다녀왔습니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

Document 253: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK스퀘어(402340) SK하이닉스 최대주주SK하이닉스 최대주주 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
SK 하이닉스 최대주주 
SK 하이닉스 4 분기도 배당 주당 20,000 원 이상  
SK 하이닉스는 전일 주당 20,000원(실적반영: 10,000원, 키옥시아 특별배당: 
10,000원)의 3분기 배당을 발표하였다. 키옥시아 특별배당은 3분기 배당과 26년 회
계연도 결산배당으로 나누어 지급예정이다. 이에 SK스퀘어는 2조 9,220억원(1분기
와 2분기 배당수입은 각 548억원)의 배당수입을 수취하게 된다. SK하이닉스는 26년 
전체 현금 배당 규모는 30조원 이상을 발표했는데, 3분기까지 지급하는 배당금은 14.6
조원이기 때문에 4분기에도 최소한 주당 20.000원 이상의 배당이 예상된다. 이 경우 
SK 스퀘어의 26 년 배당수입은 6 조원에 달할 것으로 예상한다. SK 스퀘어는 ‘26년
~’28년 경상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 주주환원정책을 보유하고 있
어 SK스퀘어의 현 시가총액 대비 27년 주주환원율은 최소 1.5% 이상 될 전망이다.

SK 하이닉스 자사주 취득·소각으로 높아질 지배력 
SK하이닉스는 20조원의 자사주 매입을 발표하였다. 이 중 10조원은 임직원 보상목적
이며, 10조원은 주주환원 목적이다. 26년 총 60조원 이상의 자사주 취득·소각 예정(8
월 결의분 40 조원 포함)으로 임직원 보상 목적의 자사주 매입은 제외되기 때문에 26
년 실적 확정 후 추가적으로 10조원 이상의 자사주 취득이 예상된다. 기 취득한 자사주 
40조원(3.3%)는 11월 5일 소각 예정으로 소각 이후 SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분
율은 20.5%(2 분기말 기준)에서 21.2%로 상승하게 된다. 이번 발표한 10 조원
(1.0%) 자사주 소각시 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 21.4%이 되며, 추가로 10 조원을 추가 
소각한다면 21.7%까지 지분율이 상승하게 된다.

SK 하이닉스 대비 높은 베타 
SK스퀘어에 대한 투자의견 매수를 유지한다. SK스퀘어 NAV의 98%가량을 차지하
는 SK하이닉스의 현금배당 증가로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 재원이 확대되고, SK하이닉
스의 자사주 취득·소각으로 지배력이 강화되기 때문이다. SK하이닉스는 전일 컨퍼런스 
콜을 통해 29년까지 D램과 HBM 물량이 사실상 모두 솔드아웃된 상황이라 밝히며 주
가 반등 가능성이 높은데, SK 스퀘어 베타(1.8)는 SK 하이닉스(1.7) 대비 높기 때문에 
SK 하이닉스 주가 반등에 따른 SK 스퀘어의 동반 주가 상승을 기대할 수 있다. 현재 
SK스퀘어의 NAV 대비 할인율은 46.5%이며, SK하이닉스가 자사주60조원 소각시 
할인율은 49.4%까지 상승하게 된다.

2026-10-29

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

COMPLIANCE NOTICE 
작성자(최관순)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도 
SK 증권 유니버스 투자등급 비율 (2026년 10 월 29 일 기준)  매수 96.48% 중립 3.52% 매도 0.00%

Document 293: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

Document 305: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

Source PDF: all report pdf/Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53 .2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities  in the coming 6 -12 months , 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15 -20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid -to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid -cycle bull market . Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities  within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook  to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) equities , which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Allocation to global equities decrease to 52.5% from 
53.2% for our CIO Foundation Balanced portfolio 
Foundation tactical asset allocation

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex -UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate  are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low -teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities . We expect the 
segment to generate 10 -15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others  consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years , with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 30 October 2026

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High -Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks , MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance  currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advance s in oncology a nd innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks  
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. H ealthcare and 
consumer staples account s for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management  in a mid -
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and p otentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge  
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

Standard Chartered Bank

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund  strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two , and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets , private equity provides  a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, i nfrastructure offers  differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where c ontracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3 -7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell -off caused by AI -
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi -sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010 -2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

Nvidia and Tesla jointly
invested Korea ABF substrate
vendor; Implications
Nvidia 與  Tesla 聯⼿注資三星電機，除了試圖打破  ABF 載板⿓頭⻑
期短缺及漲價壟斷，是否也為了採⽤三星 2nm 晶圓代⼯鋪路 ?
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS

OCT 23 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
What’s new?
2026 年  10 ⽉  22 ⽇⾄  23 ⽇，路透社（ Reuters ）、南韓《每⽇經濟新聞》
（ Maeil Business Newspaper ）與多家科技產業媒體同步披露：全球  AI 晶
⽚霸主輝達（ NVIDIA ）與電動⾞及具⾝智能巨頭特斯拉（ Tesla ），聯⼿向南
韓三星電機（ Samsung Electro-Mechanics ）注資約  28 億美元（折合約
3.7 兆韓元），⽤於其在南韓世宗市（ Sejong ）⼯廠⼤規模新建專⽤⾼階覆晶
球柵陣列（ FC-BGA / ABF Substract ）半導體封裝載板產線。此前幾天（ 10
⽉  19 ⽇），三星電機已於官⽅聲明中正式確認與全球⼤型企業達成戰略夥伴協
議，將⾃  2027 年 1 ⽉ 1 ⽇起正式向⼤客戶供應⾼階半導體封裝載板（三星电机
与全球⼤型企业签订 “ 存储器半导体⽤封装基板 ” ⻓期供应合同（ LTA ））。報導

指出，在  28 億美元注資總承諾中，輝達預計出資約  2.6 兆韓元，特斯拉出資
約  1.1 兆韓元。這筆巨額資⾦旨在為輝達下⼀代  Blackwell Ultra GB300
NVL72 超級計算叢集、 Rubin 架構  GPU ，以及特斯拉即將量產的  HW5 ⾃動
駕駛⾞載晶⽚與  Optimus 機器⼈神經網絡晶⽚，鎖定最為緊缺的⾼層數、⼤
⾯積先進封裝載板產能。
付費訂閱戶可分享受惠，受害公司 / 產業，⾮共識想
法，及建議
Subscribed

### Page 2

Positively aﬀect what supply chain vendors and
explains why?
三星電機  (Samsung Electro-Mechanics, 009150.KS) ：獲得輝達與
特斯拉兩⼤全球最⼤  AI 算⼒買家的巨額資本與⻑約鎖定，使其⼀舉打破傳統
⽇系（ Ibiden, Shinko) 與台系廠商 ( 欣興，南電）的⻑期壟斷，躍升為全球
AI 載板⼀線核⼼主⼒。
ABF 味之素積層膜材料商（⽇本味之素  Ajinomoto ）與載板設備商：⼤⾯
積  FC-BGA 載板層數已⾼達  20 層以上，良率爬坡極耗材料，直接帶動上游特
種介電樹脂與雷射鑽孔設備採購放量。
Negatively aﬀect what supply chain vendors and
explains why?
傳統載板雙寡頭（⽇本揖斐電  Ibiden 、新光電氣  Shinko ）及台系傳統載板
廠（欣興、南電）：輝達與特斯拉主動扶持三星電機建⽴新載板產能，短期可

能看不出來，但⻑期將削弱現有⽇台供應商過去兩年在⾼階載板定價上的絕對
話語權與超額利潤率，並改變市場份額。以 28 億美元投資來看，雖然僅佔 4%
的三星電機市值，但確明顯超過  Ibiden 及欣興近 10-15 億美元的年度資本開
⽀。
晶圓代⼯⿓頭台積電及封測產業鏈：從 Nvidia 及 Tesla 在 HBM DRAM ， DDR
DRAM ， NAND 與三星電⼦的採購關係，擴⼤到三星電機  ABF 載板產能擴充
的注資，我們不排除  Nvidia 及 Tesla 將進⼀步採⽤三星電⼦ 2nm （近期良率
有明顯改善）晶圓代⼯製程及封測技術，讓三星集團進⾏  AI 訓練及推理晶⽚
製造整合服務來搶台廠份額。
未獲得載板保證的⼆線  ASIC 晶⽚設計公司：頂級載板產能被巨頭包攬，使資
⾦鏈較弱的中⼩  AI 晶⽚公司⾯臨載板交期延⻑⾄  50 週以上的斷鏈危機。
Our outside the box non-consensus thinking
特斯拉參投載板是「實體  AI 製造去台化 / 去⽇化」的戰略預演：市場多將注意

⼒集中於輝達，但特斯拉⾸次拿出  1.1 兆韓元直投載板廠更具震撼性。這證明
⾺斯克已深刻意識到 ——HW5 晶⽚與百萬台  Optimus 的真正死⽳不僅僅是
晶圓代⼯產能短缺，⽽也包括 ABF 封裝載板⾯積及厚度激增引發的良率瓶頸。
特斯拉直接以⾞企⾝分下場穿透供應鏈投資底層零組件，正在重構半導體採購
範式。
「晶⽚⾯積膨脹」倒逼載板成為半導體新護城河：隨著晶⽚尺⼨光罩極限朝
10x 以上突破（ Reticle Limit ），單顆  GB300 或  HW5 載板尺⼨已超過
100mm x 100mm ，相當於⼀塊⼩型主機板。載板製造已從傳統  PCB 加⼯
轉變為「類晶圓級微影⼯程」，載板產能的鎖定甚⾄⽐搶奪台積電  CoWoS 配
額更具決定性。

### Page 3

Our advises to investors
我們建議投資⼈關注三星電機（ Samsung Electro-Mechanics ）及其核⼼材
料設備供應商在產業份額的變化，尤其是在輝達與特斯拉雙重認證下，其⾼階
載板營收佔⽐將在未來兩年由不⾜  15% 是否能飆升⾄  30% 以上，迎來估值
重估。適度獲利了結估值處於歷史⾼點的傳統純載板標的，防範  2027 年三星
電機新產能開出後，⾏業整體⽑利率⾯臨均值回歸的競爭壓⼒。三星電機產線
在⼤⾯積  FC-BGA 的翹曲控制（ Warpage Control ）與熱膨脹良率爬坡仍值
得觀察。

Document 373: Policy, politics and earnings

Source PDF: all report pdf/Policy, politics and earnings.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Weekly Market Views)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

WS Global CIO Office 
23 October 2026

Policy, politics  
and earnings

 We retain our defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, 
sectors and regions amid near-term 
uncertainty around monetary policy, 
politics and earnings. 
 The next two weeks will be busy for 
major central banks, US political 
watchers and technology leaders 
powering the AI supercycle. We expect 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold rates 
next week, but markets will focus on 
whether central banks are turning more 
hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices.

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers 
Consensus S&P500 index Q3 earnings estimates by sector   Chance of Democrats winning control of US House and Senate*

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *Polymarket probability

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary:  We retain our near -term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid -term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3 -7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15 -year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US?  Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities  among all sc enarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer -term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fa llible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bond s (CoCos1) 
and US 10 -year go vernment bond ideas:  We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10 -year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:   Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share -price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monet isation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence.  We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight , while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral  and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent  technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long -term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro  area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro  area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While Fr ance’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro  
area bank equities  offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro  area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5 -3.0% q/q , led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased , despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs . Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro  area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited , supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in  China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as  a Core holding , as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in  AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weak ened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sect or contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation , leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk -reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10 -year yield fell  over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though.  Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y . Fed officials  have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

Significant share issuance has kept the market 
capitalisation fairly stable, while the MSCI China 
Index has declined c.20% over the past one year

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds , especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell -off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals . The sustained widening of the OAT -Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near -term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating rev iews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the  French presidential election . Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near -term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe -haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The Fr ance-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to  constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves  the CHF at a sizeable interest -
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90 , excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention . A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near -term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next  12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in Fr ench government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high  public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustment s are  weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s  10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premi a on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, e levated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi -year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid -2027, reducing USD support and driving  the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk  is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding  conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

France’s fiscal risks constrain near-term EUR/USD 
gains, but a modest recovery remains likely

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our 
December quarter CPI to 1.3%qtr/3.9%yr 
and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the 
quarterly estimate. Outside of a 0.09ppt contribution to the 
revision from holiday travel & accommodation (revised up from 
4.2% to 5.6%) all else was via small contributions for a large 
number of expenditure categories. This is why there is a 0.2ppt 
increase in our September quarter TM estimate from 0.7%qtr 
to 0.9%qtr.
A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

to about pre-crisis levels by end 2027, a combination of a slow 
recovery in refining capacity, constrained return of physical 
supplies and higher shipping cost keep Australian fuel prices 
above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve 
fast than we expect, and auto fuel prices return to pre-conflict 
level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

Document 515: バーナム新政権も財政拡張路線を堅持

Source PDF: all report pdf/バーナム新政権も財政拡張路線を堅持.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 6 日

MURC Focus

バーナム新政権も財政拡張路線を堅持

～10 月末発表の秋季予算案で具体的な財源を示せるか～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 伝統的な財政拡張を訴えるバーナム新政権 
英国では、9 月 27 日から 30 日にかけて、北西部の主要都市リバプールで与党である労働党の党大会が開催
された。7 月 20 日に就任したアンディ・バーナム首相にとって初の党大会となると同時に、新政権の財政運営方
針が示されるという点で、この党大会は非常に高い注目を浴びた。この場で示されたのは、中道左派である労働
党らしい、伝統的な財政拡張路線に基づくプランだった。

具体的には、バーナム 新政権の関係閣僚らは、政府調達（政府が民間からモノやサービスを購入すること）の
増強による景気浮揚策を主張した。例えばジョン・ヒーリー財務相は 28 日の党大会で、スコットランドのクライド海
軍基地に浮きドック 3 基を建設する他、海洋調査船を建造することに数十億ポンドを投資する計画を発表した。
いずれも国内の業者に発注することで雇用や所得につなげる方針である。

また労働党大会に先立つ 26 日、バーナム新政権は、新築住宅を初めて購入する国民向けに新たな住宅購
入支援策を導入すると発表した。保守党政権下の 2013 年から 2023 年にかけて実施された“Help to Buy”と似
たスキームであり、購入者は購入価格の 2.5%に相当する頭金を支払えば、政府が購入価格の 20%までを融資
するというものである。

このように、バーナム新政権が目指す方針は、労働党に伝統的な“大きな政府”による財政拡張路線を反映し
ている。そうした点で、バーナム新政権の方針はキア・スターマー前政権とはあまり変化がない。一方で、新政権
が示した財政拡張路線が金融市場の評価を得るためには、財源を明示する必要がある。10 月 28 日に発表する
秋季予算案で、金融市場が納得する内容を示せるかが鍵を握る。

〇9月末にリバプールで開催された労働党の党大会で、バーナム新政権の財政運営方針が示されたが 、それ
は中道左派である労働党らしい伝統的な財政拡張路線に基づくプランだった。 
〇一方、金融市場 では、それまではバーナム 人気によって労働党 の政権運営が安定するとの思惑から“英国
買い”が先行していたが、党大会前後から調整色を濃くしている。 
〇増税は有権者の反発を、国債発行が投資家の反発を生むと予想される中、秋季予算で財政拡張の財源の
筋道をきちんと示せなければ、バーナム新政権も短命で終わりかねない。

### Page 2

2. 財政拡張を続けてきた近年の英国 
バーナム新政権は財政拡張に活路を 見いだそうとしているが、これまでも英国の政権は財政拡張に臨んでき
た（図表 1）。例えば英国の実質 GDP（国内総生産）に占める公需（＝政府支出＋公的固定資本形成）は、保守
党政権（テリーザ・メイ元首相）期の 2018 年後半に 21.5%を下回るまで低下したが、以降は上昇し、直近 2026
年 4～6 月時点で 24.4%となっている（図表 1）。

この間、2020 年 1～3 月期に生じたコロナショック後の公需の減退も限定的にとどまっており、結局は公需が
GDP に占める割合は“V 字回復”することとなった。労働党が政権を奪取したのは 2024 年 7 月の総選挙だが、
それ以前の保守党政権下でも、英国は事実上、財政拡張を続けていたことになる。その結果、公的債務残高の
対 GDP 比率も、一時は高インフレのために低下したが、再び上昇した（図表 2）。

このように近年の英国は、保革の立場を問わず財政拡張を続けてきた。しかし、EU 離脱やエネルギー不安に
伴う供給制約の高まりの中でそうした手法を取り続けたため 、英国はスタグフレーション（景気停滞と物価高進の
併存）に突入した。その改善のために必要な処方箋は、規制緩和を通じた供給の刺激である。しかし、それは“小
さな政府”を追求することにもなるため、中道左派として“大きな政府”を目指す労働党は容認できない。

バーナム新首相は地方の主導の経済成長を訴え、そうした意味での分権化に意欲的と考えられる。 ただし労
働党としては、公需による民需の刺激を通じた景気浮揚を引き続き志向していることが、秋の党大会で示された
形である。一方、金融市場では、バーナム新首相就任直後は、首相の人気に支えられて新政権の運営が安定す
るとの思惑から“英国買い”が先行していたが、党大会前後から調整色を濃くしている。

### Page 3

3. 財源なき財政拡張で信用を失うリスク 
スターマー前政権もまた、財政拡張路線による景気浮揚を目指した が、同時にそれは、供給の底上げを狙うも
のでもあった。老朽インフラ（鉄道や水道など）の再開発はその最たるものである。またスターマー前政権は、財政
規律にも配慮し、財源を国債発行よりも増税でカバーし、 財政拡張策の整合性を取ろう とした。財源を示したこと
を投資家は評価したが、増税に有権者が反発したため、結局、スターマー前政権は短命に終わった。

スターマー前政権下で低迷した労働党の支持率は、バーナム人気に支えられ“V 字回復”し、右派の改革党
（UK Reform ）と肉薄するに至る（図表 3）。労働党としては打ち出した目玉政策を着実に遂行し、有権者の支持
をさらに確たるものにしたいところである。とはいえ、そうした目玉政策は財政拡張を伴うものであるため、財源をど
う確保するか明確な筋道を示さなければ、金融市場の動揺を誘う。

すでに英国の長期金利（10 年国債流通利回り）は、グローバルなインフレ加速懸念に伴う世界的な金利上昇
の波に飲まれ、上昇が続いている（図表 4）。金融市場は財政拡張に対して神経を尖らせているが、秋季予算で
明確な財源が示さなければ、それを嫌気した投資家が英国債（ギルト債）の売却を進め、金利が一段と上昇し、
場合によってポンド安と株安の“トリプル安”を引き起こす可能性もある。

財政拡張の財源を増税で賄おうとすると、スターマー前政権のように有権者の支持を失いかねない。 一方、国
債発行で賄おうとすると、長期金利の押し上げ圧力になる。ゆえに、歳出の見直しで財源を賄おうとするだろうが、
簡単ではないため、具体的な筋道を示せなければ、結局は金利の上昇につながる。秋季予算で財政拡張の財
源の筋道をきちんと示せなければ、バーナム新政権も短命で終わりかねない。
